**Book 115:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Iqbal Helmi, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-909-6. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 116:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 5 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–14, stating the Fase C outcomes, which cover more than this Grade 5 book alone) plus one guide section per student chapter, with full answer keys (a 0–4 scoring rubric per test item), misconception notes, differentiation, remedial and enrichment tasks, parent interaction and reading lists (printed pp. 1–428). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 2 answer key (printed p. 89) answers 15:30 + 6 minutes as 15:41; the correct sum is 15:36 — taught corrected. The glossary shared by both Grade 5 books defines KPK as the greatest common multiple; KPK is the smallest common multiple, as the chapter teaching itself states — taught corrected. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Even and odd numbers; divisibility in the times table — if pure water packed in 2-litre vessels is bought (3, 4, 5 vessels), how many litres of water will there be? Mark it on the number ray and complete the table (the number line is marked in vessels and litres; the table: the number of vessels 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 10 against the measure in litres 4, 6, … — a proportional table); discuss the property of numbers divisible by two and of numbers not divisible by two, and complete the conclusion (a girl's note: "Numbers divisible by two are called …… numbers" — even numbers, тэгш); discuss in how many repetitions an odd number makes an even number, and solve (writing Т for an even number and С for an odd number: Т + Т = ?; С + С = ?; С × С = ?; Т × С = ?; the worked solutions: 10 = С + С; 12 = 2 × С; 12 = □ × С; 50 = С + С; 50 = □ × С; the conclusion sentence: "An odd number multiplied by a …… number gives a …… number"); write the expression that fits the pattern and draw the conclusion (a table to complete for Т + Т, Т + С, С + С, Т × Т, Т × С, С × С, Т − Т, Т − С, С − С and С − Т — the parity of each result); an activity: work following the instructions, using the multiplication table: a. circle the numbers divisible by two; b. colour the numbers divisible by five yellow: among the two-digit numbers, how many are there that divide by 5?; c. write the numbers divisible by 2 and by 5 side by side from the smallest, and circle the biggest; d. find a number divisible by 10: multiply it by the column number and by the row number and write it (the example: 20 is (5 × 4), (4 × 5), (2 × 10) and (10 × 2)); e. write the numbers divisible by 4 in your notebook; f. write the numbers whose divisor is 4 in your notebook; g. find the numbers that are divided by both numbers; mark the values of the products of equal numbers (✓); a self-check: check each product by the quotient and explain the dependence: find the factors, where the same shape in the picture stands for the same number (20 = ● × □; 20 = ● × ● × ▲; 20 = ◆ × ▲) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 26 |
| Writing a sum as a product (Нийлбэрийг үржвэрээр бичих): discuss the dependence of the multiplication and division actions: a. six groups packed 24 apples equally: into how many boxes did each group pack them? The fewest boxes were used (pictures of 24 arranged six ways: as 4 × 6, as two 4 × 4 blocks wait — the arrays show the equal groupings of 24: 6 + 6 + 6 + 6, 8 + 8 + 8, twelve 2s in a grid, 12 + 12); b. write the divisors (factors) of the number 24 and circle the biggest one; compose problems for finding the number of pieces, or for finding each one's share, and discuss them (a. the repeated additions 4 + 4 + 4; 3 + 3 + 3 + 3; 5 + 5 + 5 + 5; 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 are written as products; b. the repeated subtractions that reach zero — 12 − 4 − 4 − 4 = 0; 15 − □ − □ − □ = 0; 32 − □ − □ − □ − □ = 0 — division as repeated subtraction); using the factors' commutative property, compose problems (the strip diagrams: 2 × 4 = □ against □ × 2 = □; 5 + 5 + 5 becomes 3 × □; 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 becomes 5 × □); compose a problem fitting the diagram and solve it (a. a bar model of two bars totalling 54, one bar divided into 3 equal parts; b. a bar model with a part of 180 and the whole unknown); using the number cards 9, 7 and 63, write an equality: discuss how one term is expressed through the other two terms; an activity: find the divisor using the number machine: the number machine carries out the action 5 + 5 = = … again and again: how does the value that comes out on the screen change? Discuss how the number of equal addends, or the number of repetitions, forms the number's divisor: how can you know how many times 5 has been added in the screen's result? Try the action 6 + 6 = = … on the screen and explain the result: by this method, find the divisors related to 7, 8 and 9 yourselves | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 27 |
| Factor trees; prime-like and composite-like numbers — breaking a number into factor products (Тоог үржигдэхүүн болгон задлах): a one-digit number broken into a product of 2 numbers is shown: discuss how many divisors each number has (the factor trees: 2 = 1 × 2; 3 = 1 × 3; 5 = 1 × 5 — a boy's note: "2, 3 and 5 are divided by 1 and by themselves"; 6 = 2 × 3; 8 = 2 × 4; 9 = 3 × 3 — a girl's note: "6, 8 and 9 are divided by 1, by themselves and by other numbers too"); a. 72 is the product of which numbers?; b. by which numbers is 72 wholly divided (three factor trees of 72: 72 = 8 × 9, broken down to the circled factors 2, 2, 3, 3; 72 = 2 × 36 → 2 × 18 → …; 72 = 6 × 12 → …)?; break the following numbers into products of 2 numbers (tables of factor pairs: for 36 — 1 × 36, 2 × 18, 3 × 12, 6 × 6; for 48 — 1 × 48, 2 × 24, … with the columns 3, 4, 8 to fill); how can the numbers 24, 48 and 76 be broken following the pattern (24 = □ × □; 24 = □ × □ × □; 24 = □ × □ × □ × □)?; compose a problem fitting the picture (a. a number line cut into equal segments of 3 with the whole unknown; b. a number line running to 72 with its marks and unknowns); write the fitting number (16 ÷ 2 ÷ 2 = 1; 15 ÷ 5 ÷ 3 = 1; 25 ÷ □ ÷ □ = 1; 30 ÷ □ ÷ □ ÷ □ = 1; 20 ÷ □ ÷ □ ÷ □ = 1; 18 ÷ □ ÷ □ = 1 — dividing down to 1 by the factor chain); a self-check: write the numbers 107, 183, 275, 48, 209, 108, 63, 309, 32 and 84 in the form of their smallest products (broken fully into prime factors); an activity: two pupils throw four dice in turn: each throw is scored by the table (Оноо өгөх хувилбар: the score depends on how the dice land — a single landing scores 15000 points, two 25000, three 35000, four 45000, five 55000, six 65000) and the totals are registered in the score table (the table: the throw number 1-4 and the total, for Pupil A and Pupil B): at the end of the game, whoever has more points is the winner: if you make a mistake saying your own score from memory, there is a 500-point penalty: calculate who won by how many points (the "score changes first" note: the points can change as the game goes on); race each other: prepare the table beforehand: a. among the numbers whose sum is 100, find 2 numbers lying in a row, a column or a diagonal and circle them as shown (a grid of numbers from 18 to 100 with pairs like 82 and 18 circled); b. among the numbers whose sum is 1000, find the two divisors of 50 lying in a row, a column or a diagonal and circle them as shown (a grid of the multiples 50, 100, 150 … 950); c. using a calculator, enter the action 50 + 50 = = … and continue it until 1000 appears on the screen: observe and discuss which numbers' divisors are appearing on the screen (the multiples of 50) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 28–29 |
| Consolidation: classifying numbers; segments on a line; equal volumes — classify the numbers by their divisibility property in the table and write them (the numbers 346; 5612; 34; 12; 123; 0; 198; 1000; 999; 52; 81; 1240; 243; 561; 510; 33; 45 — the table crossing even (Тэгш) and odd (Сондгой) with divisible by 3 and not divisible by 3); how many segments are there in the picture (the points A, B, C, D, E and F on one line: 15 segments)?; tell the number of the shape that has no symmetry about the line (the arrow, the star, the heart and the pentagon, each with its dashed line); find the coloured shape's area measure (counting the unit squares: a. write the coloured part's area measure compared with the uncoloured part's area measure as a ratio; b. write the coloured part's area measure compared with the whole shape's area measure as a ratio — the five pictured grid shapes); make a rectangular shape out of a wire 32 cm long: if the rectangle's width is 4 cm, what is its length in centimetres (12 cm)?; the three parallelepipeds' volumes are equal: find the lengths of the edges a and c (the first solid 4 cm by 5 cm by 8 cm = 160 cm.kub; the second 8 cm by 5 cm by a, so a = 4 cm; the third 4 cm by 4 cm by c, so c = 10 cm); a puzzle: can 1 triangle be built from 3 iron wires 6 cm, 8 cm and 2 cm long? Explain your answer (no: 6 + 2 is not greater than 8); the livestock counts of the five kinds of a wealthy herder (Итгэлт баян) are marked in the following table: find how many there are of each kind (the pictograph in which 100 head of livestock are marked by a whole circle picture and 50 by a half: the horses (Адуу), camels (Тэмээ), cattle (Үхэр), sheep (Хонь) and goats (Ямаа) rows of whole and half circles to be counted in hundreds and fifties) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 144 |
| Divisors and multiples of a number; common divisors found with Euler–Venn diagrams | Mongolia, Математик V (Grade 5), printed p. 32 |
| Common multiples and least common multiple in problems | Mongolia, Математик V (Grade 5), printed p. 34 |
| Divisibility tests for 2, 4, 5, 10, 25, 50, 100 derived from multiplication tables | Mongolia, Математик V (Grade 5), printed p. 35 |
| Divisibility tests extended: tests for 4, 6, 8 worked out and applied | Mongolia, Математик VI (Grade 6), printed p. 12 |
| Greatest common divisor (HCF) of two numbers in context problems | Mongolia, Математик VI (Grade 6), printed p. 13 |
| Factorising into prime factors by factor tree and by column division; prime factorisation in index form (24 = 2³ × 3) | Mongolia, Математик VII (Grade 7), printed p. 13 |
| GCD and LCM from prime factorisations: GCD takes the smaller powers, LCM the larger (24, 36 → 12, 72) | Mongolia, Математик VII (Grade 7), printed p. 14 |

| Multiples — múltiplos of 2 including 0 as the múltiplo nulo; all even numbers are multiples of 2; multiples of 5 end in 0 or 5; numbers ending in 0 are multiples of 2 and of 5 | Venezuela, Aventuras de Patacalientes (Grade 3), printed pp. 104–107 |
| Divisors — reading a division table from the ×2 table; divisor and divisible language: "si yo soy tu múltiplo, tú eres mi divisor"; min/max multiple and divisor questions | Venezuela, Aventuras de Patacalientes (Grade 3), printed pp. 108–113 |
| Divisors — sharing 12 polvorosas into 1–12 bags: exact only for 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 12 = the divisores de 12; 18 suspiros give divisores 1, 2, 3, 6, 9, 18; exact division has remainder 0 and Dividendo = Divisor × Cociente; the fundamental relation with remainder < divisor recalled | Venezuela, Contando con los recursos (Grade 4), printed pp. 148–151 |
| Common divisors and GCD — common divisors of 12 and 18 in a Venn-style diagram; el máximo común divisor = 6, with formal methods deferred to 5th grade | Venezuela, Contando con los recursos (Grade 4), printed pp. 152–154 (corrected at citation check: the common divisors are built on pp. 152–153, and the naming "en el caso del 12 y el 18 es el 6, suele llamarse MÁXIMO COMÚN DIVISOR" is on p. 154, PDF p. 79) |
| Multiples — firms meeting every 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 days marked on a January 2011 calendar: the meeting dates are múltiplos of 2 (15 dates), of 3 (10 dates: 3, 6, …, 30), of 4, of 5; multiple defined in the pupils' own words | Venezuela, Contando con los recursos (Grade 4), printed pp. 154–157 |
| Divisors — Divisors of the first twelve naturals tabulated; classifying numbers by divisor count (exactly one, exactly two, more than two) | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 77–79 |
| Primes — Prime and composite defined by divisor count; 1 is neither | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed p. 79 |
| Primes — Sieve of Eratosthenes over 1–100, step by step | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 80–81 |
| Primes — Even numbers as sums of two primes (Goldbach activity, conjecture named) | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed p. 82 |
| Factorisation — Prime factorisation built factor by factor (12 = 2² × 3; 80 = 2⁴ × 5) and the uniqueness conclusion: every composite has exactly one prime factorisation | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 82–83 |
| LCM — LCM introduced from meeting-day cycles: lcm(2,3,4,5,6) = 60 by listing multiples | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 84–85 |
| GCD — GCD introduced from making identical packages: gcd(36,48,72) = 12 by listing common divisors | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed p. 85 |
| GCD — Euclid's algorithm worked in full: gcd(1.944, 4.608) = 72, then bag weights 1.944 ÷ 72 = 27 kg and 4.608 ÷ 72 = 64 kg | Venezuela, La Patria Buena (Grade 5), printed pp. 86–87 |
| LCM by listing — LCM by listing multiples: pill times every 12, 8 and 6 hours coincide at lcm(12,8,6) = 24 | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed pp. 85–86 |
| LCM by ladder — LCM by simultaneous decomposition (ladder): lcm(25,20,15) = 5 × 5 × 2 × 2 × 3 = 300 | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed p. 87 |
| Divisors — Divisors from exact sharing: the exact packings of 36 pills give the divisors of 36 (1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36); dividend = divisor × quotient + remainder, remainder < divisor, exact when remainder 0 | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed pp. 88–89 |
| GCD — GCD named as the greatest common divisor (of 36, 48, 54) | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed p. 90 |
| Divisibility — Divisibility criteria for 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 stated and applied (digit sum for 3; last two digits for 4; by 2 and 3 for 6) | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed pp. 90–91 |
| Factorisation — Prime factorisation by the ladder: 48 = 2⁴ × 3; 60 = 2² × 3 × 5 | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed pp. 91–92 |
| LCM/GCD — LCM and GCD from factorisations: lcm(48,60) = 2⁴ × 3 × 5 = 240 (highest powers), gcd(48,60) = 2² × 3 = 12 (lowest powers of common factors) | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed pp. 92–93 |
| Simplifying — Simplifying a fraction by the GCD: 48/60 = 4/5 after dividing both terms by 12 | Venezuela, Hecho en Venezuela (Grade 6), printed p. 93 |
| GCD — Greatest common divisor by prime factorisation (common factors with their lowest exponents): gcd(2.000, 120) = 40 gives the largest cloth squares for the wiphala | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed pp. 53–54 |
| LCM — Least common multiple by prime factorisation (common and non-common factors with their highest exponents): lcm(10, 6) = 30 for two runners meeting again | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed pp. 54–55 |
| Primes — Prime and composite numbers defined by their divisors; the number 1 is neither | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed pp. 56–58 |
| Sieve — Sieve of Eratosthenes worked on a 7 × 7 wiphala grid of 1–49 and extended as an activity to the first 200 naturals | Venezuela, Matemática Para La Vida (Grade 7), printed pp. 55–58 |

**Sub lesson map (added 2026-11-08, whole-arc rule):** the
rows above already stocked the divisibility tests and the
Euclidean GCD; the text now teaches them. Sub lesson 2
(Divisibility Tests) is carried by the Ethiopia Grade 6 row
(rules for 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, ch. 1), the South Sudan
rows (Pupil's Book 3: 2, 5, 10; Pupil's Book 5: 3, 4, 6, 9;
Pupil's Book 6: 8 and 11), the Yemen Grade 4 row (10, 5, 2
and 3), the Armenia row (Chapter 5 itself named
"Divisibility") and the Tajikistan rows (3 and 9 by the
digit sum, p.15). The successive-division method in Sub
lesson 4 is carried by the Tajikistan row (worked scheme
GCD(195, 104) = 13, p.22) and the Laos Grade 6 row (GCD by
the Euclidean algorithm). No existing row changed.

**Acceptance count: taught in 68 countries (Egypt, Armenia, Jordan, Libya, Kazakhstan, Greece, Vietnam, South Africa, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, North Macedonia, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Tajikistan, Turkey, Tanzania, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Eritrea, Chile, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Peru, Suriname, Papua New Guinea, Timor-Leste, Uganda)**. Egypt carries the whole topic twice — prime and composite with GCF in Grade 4, prime factorisation with GCF and LCM in Grade 5, including choosing between them in word problems. Armenia carries divisibility whole in Grade 5 — divisor and multiple, GCD, LCM, primes, factorisation. Jordan carries factors and multiples with GCF and LCM in Grade 5. Libya carries the topic in Grade 7 — prime factors, HCF, multiples, LCM. Kazakhstan carries GCD and LCM in Grade 5. Greece generalises powers, GCD/LCM and primes in Grade 6. Vietnam carries divisors, multiples, primes, GCD and LCM in Grade 6. South Africa carries primes first as a coloured 1–100 grid in Grade 5 and prime/composite with factors of 1,000,000 in Grade 6. Other read countries teach factors inside their own Grade 5–7 books and join by merge.** Laos carries multiples and factors in Grade 4, primes to 19 with divisibility rules in Grade 5, and the full GCD/LCM course — listing, Euclidean algorithm and factorisation — in Grade 6. Yemen carries divisibility rules for 2, 5, 10 and 3 in Grade 4, then factors, primes, prime factorisation, GCD and LCM in Grade 5. Indonesia carries factors and multiples first in Grade 4, the full LCM/GCD course — multiples, factors, primes, the Sieve of Eratosthenes, prime factorisation and LCM/GCD by listing, factorisation and repeated division — in Grade 5, and FPB/KPK again with prime factorisation in Grade 7 (Merdeka and K13 Semester 1). Two book errors are never taught as fact: the Grade 5 student book's worked FPB(75, 150) = 6 on printed p. 59 (the true value is 75), and both Grade 5 books' glossaries define KPK as the greatest common multiple (KPK is the smallest common multiple; the chapter teaching on student p. 40 is correct). The Grade 7 K13 successive-division ladder for FPB(24, 48, 72) on printed p. 97 gives 12; the true value is 24. Nicaragua carries multiples, divisors, LCM, GCD and primes in Grade 5, prime factorisation with GCD and LCM by the prime-factor method in Grade 6, and divisibility criteria with LCM/GCD again in the Grade 7 module. Ethiopia carries the whole topic in Grade 6 — divisibility rules for 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9 and 10, even and odd numbers, multiples and factors, prime and composite numbers with 1 neither prime nor composite, prime factorisation with factor trees, and HCF and LCM by listing and by prime factorisation; Grade 9 revises the whole topic — divisibility tests, primes, prime factorisation, GCF and LCM — in its number-system unit. South Sudan carries divisibility tests for 2, 5 and 10 in Grade 3, multiples and factors in Grade 4, divisibility tests for 3, 4, 6 and 9 with primes, factors and HCF/LCM in Grade 5, divisibility tests of 8 and 11 in Grade 6, factors and multiples including negatives in Grade 8, and factors with prime/composite numbers and HCF/LCM by prime factors in Secondary 1. One book slip is never taught as fact: the Grade 6 book's p. 5 divisibility-of-8 example tests by 4 on the wrong digits mid-working — cite the rule, not that working. Iraq carries divisibility by 2, 3, 5 and 10 with factors and multiples in Grade 4, then primes, factorisation in exponential form, perfect squares and cubes, and GCD and LCM in Grade 5. Bangladesh carries the whole topic from Class 4 — multiples, factors, LCM, HCF, primes with the sieve to 100 and prime factorisation with divisibility by 2, 3 and 5 — through LCM, GCF and factor trees in Class 5, divisibility rules, prime factorisation, the HCF game and the Euclidean method in Class 6, to GCD and LCM of fractions and of algebraic expressions in Class 7. Nepal carries the whole topic from prime and composite numbers with prime factors in Grade 4 — teach the p. 23 list corrected, 1 is not prime — through prime factorization in Grade 5, to factors, multiples, prime factorization, HCF and LCM in Grade 6 (teach the common prime factors of 450 and 240 corrected: 2, 3 and 5), and HCF and LCM again in Grade 7. Grade 3 teaches no prime numbers. Cambodia carries factors, multiples, GCD and LCM by prime factorisation in Grades 6–7 and again in Grade 12 Advanced, with the prime definition there; an explicit prime-versus-composite classification and any prime-listing or sieve is not pinned in the set, and the Grade 12 chapter does not teach prime factorisation as a method. Kuwait carries factors first inside its Grade 4 division unit (exploring factors; divisibility by 2, 5 and 10) and then as its own Grade 6 number-theory unit — factors, multiples, prime and composite numbers, and GCF and LCM in simple form. Iran carries the topic from divisibility in Grade 6 through divisors, highest common factor and least common multiple in Grade 7 and the sieve of Eratosthenes in Grade 8, up to divisibility and congruences in Grade 12 Discrete Mathematics. North Macedonia enters from its main set — Grade 3 (2023), Grade 5 (2022) and Grade 6 (2023).

Ghana also contributes to this lesson as curriculum and teacher evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook — and Ghana is not counted in the taught-in figure above. (Ghana rows stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.)

China carries factors, multiples and primes whole in Grade 5 Volume 2 — definitions, the divisibility rules for 2, 5 and 3, primes and the sieve, the highest common factor and the lowest common multiple.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries factors, multiples and primes in Grades 4–6 — divisibility rules and prime factorisation in Grade 4, the HCF and LCM methods in the Class 5 book, and the full treatment with the sieve of Eratosthenes in Class 6.

Syria builds factors in two passes — multiples, fact families and the least common multiple by listing in Grades 4–5, then the full prime-factorisation machinery in Grade 6: divisibility rules, ladder factorisation, GCD and LCM from factorisations, coprime numbers — and returns in Grade 9 Algebra with divisors of integers, the GCD's properties, and the successive-subtraction and Euclid algorithms.

Saudi Arabia introduces factors and multiples inside its Grade 4 multiplication chapter — every factor pair of a number listed, multiples read off the table, and the two-way fact that a number is a multiple of each of its factors stated plainly.

Malaysia carries factors and multiples whole in Form 1 — factors, prime factorisation and HCF first, then multiples and LCM — with all three methods (listing, prime factorisation, repeated division) taught for both HCF and LCM.

Rwanda teaches factors, multiples and primes from P4 — odd and even numbers, multiples, factors, prime and composite numbers, and LCM by listing and by the prime-factor ladder — through prime factorisation and the divisibility tests for 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 12 in P5, to LCM and GCF by listing and by prime factorisation, with word problems and the product rule, in P6.

Sudan meets even and odd numbers in Grade 1 (a set shared into two equal groups is even), multiples in Grade 3, and gives Grade 4 a full divisibility suite — primes, tests for 2, 3, 5 and 10, prime factorisation, HCF and LCM — extended in Grade 5 by discovery lessons for 9, 4, 8 and 6, and returns to prime factorisation in Grade 9.

India carries factors-multiples-and-primes through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 4–5 and Mathematics Class 6, 8, 10 — including a number divisible by 2, 3 and 5; multiples; factors as exact divisors, found by arranging objects in equal rows; divisibility tests.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик IV, V, VI, VII (Grades 4, 5, 6, 7): even and odd numbers; divisibility in the times table; writing a sum as a product; factors of 24; factor trees; prime-like and composite-like numbers.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6) and *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7). From its books: from Grade 3: multiples — múltiplos of 2 including 0 as the múltiplo nulo; all even numbers are multiples of 2; multiples of 5 end in 0 or 5; numbers ending in 0 are multiples of 2 and of 5; from Grade 5: divisors — Divisors of the first twelve naturals tabulated; classifying numbers by divisor count (exactly one, exactly two, more than two); from Grade 7: GCD — Greatest common divisor by prime factorisation (common factors with their lowest exponents): gcd(2.000, 120) = 40 gives the largest cloth squares for the wiphala.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 6: divisibility tests for 2 and 3; divisibility tests for 4 and 5; divisibility test for 7; divisibility test for 9; divisibility test for 10; divisibility test for 11; prime and composite numbers; factors of a number; splitting a number into factors; prime factorisation; prime factorisation by the ladder (repeated-division) method; from Grade 7: divisibility rules for natural numbers; prime numbers and composite numbers; prime factorisation of a natural number; greatest common divisor (GCD); finding GCD by factorisation; least common multiple (LCM); finding LCM by prime factorisation; using LCM and GCD to solve everyday problems; relation between LCM and GCD of two numbers; finding LCM and GCD simultaneously (table/division method); chapter summary and exercises (natural numbers).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Factors; multiples; divisors of a number; divisibility rules (2, 5, 10, 100); prime and composite numbers. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Multiples, factors, prime/composite numbers, prime factorisation of numbers > 20, common multiples (plans…. from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .5 Multiples and factors: LCM and HCF work (plan 5). from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Greatest common divisor and least common multiple by prime factors and Venn diagrams (ditare answers….

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 6: Divisors and multiples of a natural number (a = b·c); Common divisors of two or more numbers; Common multiples of numbers; Divisibility tests for 10 and for 5; Divisibility tests for 3 and for 9 by the digit sum; Prime and composite numbers; Resolving a natural number into prime factors; GCD from prime factorisations (smallest powers of common primes); GCD by the second method: successive division (Euclidean algorithm), worked scheme for…; Least common multiple (LCM) and methods of finding it, incl. prime factorisation; Historical note: the Pythagorean school and perfect numbers 6, 28, 496; Divisibility facts for integers, with proofs: sums/differences, the product fact, aⁿ…; from Grade 7: Prime values of 6p-1 and 6p+1 tables.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 2 syllabus requires, in a full body section, distinguishing factors from multiples, the factors of whole numbers, prime numbers, composite numbers, the Highest Common Factor and the Lowest Common Multiple; Class 3 keeps prime and composite numbers, factors and multiples in the scope and sequence, with the highest common factor and lowest common multiple in the body; Class 4 adds expressing a given number as a product of its prime factors (primes to 100 in the targets) and the HCF and LCM of no more than three numbers. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 6: factor (çarpan) of a natural number introduced through rectangle areas with natural-number sides (12 m² plots); a number written as a product of two natural numbers; definition: the two numbers multiplied to give a natural number are its factors; worked identification for 15 (1, 3, 5, 15); smallest factor of any (non-zero) natural number is 1, largest is the number itself; factors are finite in number — discovered by modelling rectangles for 27, 46, 80; factors are exactly the exact divisors (bölenler): a factor divides the number with no remainder; divisor defined from factor on a 1–50 grid for 50; worked examples: number with fewest/most divisors among 9, 42, 55, 11; equal-sharing days from divisors of 32; sum of divisors of 60 as a money total; problem type: cutting rods of 28, 35, 60 cm into equal natural-number lengths — number of different piece lengths = number of divisors; multiples (katlar) built from repeated addition in a savings table (5 TL per week); multiples as a number pattern; definition: multiples obtained by multiplying a natural number by the counting numbers 1, 2, 3, … (15 → 15, 30, 45, …); multiples never end; smallest multiple of a non-zero number is the number itself (hundred-grid marking for 8 and 13); contrast: factors finite, multiples infinite; largest factor = smallest multiple = the number; worked examples: multiples under 200 of the number whose factors are 1, 2, 4, 5, 10, 20; rest stops every 80 km on a 940 km road; divisibility rules discovered, not given: hundred-grid packing problems (2, 5, 10 kg sacks) lead pupils to state the criterion for each divisor; rules stated: divisible by 2 if units digit 0, 2, 4, 6, 8; by 5 if units digit 0 or 5; by 10 if units digit 0; identification exercises incl. numbers divisible by both 2 and 5; divisibility by 3 and by 9 discovered from digit sums of marked multiples on the hundred grid (12 → 1+2 = 3; 18 → 1+8 = 9); rule stated: digit sum a multiple of 3 → divisible by 3; digit sum a multiple of 9 → divisible by 9; missing-digit largest-value problem and identification sets; divisibility by 6 built as both-2-and-3: numbers marked as multiples of both 2 and 3 on the grid are the multiples of 6; rule stated and applied; divisibility by 4 discovered from the pattern of marked multiples past 100; rule stated: last two digits 00 or a multiple of 4; identification set; prime number motivated by rectangle tilings: floor areas 2–9 m² that can only be tiled in a single row; definition: a number with exactly two different natural-number factors; Sieve of Eratosthenes performed on the hundred grid in five steps (keep 2, 3, 5, 7, cross out their multiples, mark the rest except 1); why 1 is not prime; 2 the only even prime; smallest prime is 2; no even prime other than 2 (stated as facts after the sieve); prime factor (asal çarpan) defined: a factor of a number that is itself prime; factors of 12, 25, 35, 44 listed, then their prime factors picked out (12 → 2 and 3); two methods for prime factors taught side by side as a pupil dialogue: factor tree (çarpan ağacı) and prime-factor algorithm / ladder division (asal çarpan algoritması), both worked on 28; writing a number as a product of its prime factors by both methods (20 = 2 × 2 × 5; practice 36, 70, 90, 56, 98, 150); worked examples: digit making 5□ a two-digit prime; largest minus smallest prime factor of 77; recovering missing numbers from a ladder diagram and from a factor tree; common multiples (ortak kat) introduced on a timeline: donors giving blood every 3 and every 4 months coincide in common-multiple months; definition by listing: multiples of 2 and of 3 listed, common ones 6, 12, 18, … named common multiples; hundred-grid colouring for the pairs (4, 6), (4, 10), (6, 10); common-multiple problems: counting common multiples of 10 and 14 between 100 and 600 (calculator allowed); equal money totals for 12 TL chocolates and 18 TL biscuits; two doctors' next joint duty day from a calendar (7-day and 9-day cycles); common divisors (ortak bölen) introduced by sharing 24 t and 18 t of pet food equally among shelters; definition by listing: divisors of 12 and of 21 listed, common ones 1 and 3 named common divisors; colouring exercises for (16, 20) and (18, 30); counting common divisors of 45 and 90; common-divisor problems: bottle sizes for 28 L and 42 L of oil; equal piece lengths cut from 20 cm and 30 cm pipes; coprime numbers (aralarında asal) defined: two natural numbers with no common divisor other than 1; motivated by 15 kg apples and 16 kg pears packed separately; example 8 and 21; word-building identification grid.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: picking out the numbers divisible by 8 from a table (its chapter summary's reversed divisor/dividend statement taught corrected); prime numbers defined by exactly two factors, 1 not prime, a sieve to 100 giving the 25 primes; factors by division and by product pairs; factor trees and prime factorisation; the greatest common factor three ways; multiples on the number line; the lowest common multiple by listing and by the prime-factor ladder.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Mathematics 4 (student); Mathematics 5 (student); Algebra 7; Algebra and Elements of Mathematical Analysis 10 (Arakelyan): divisibility tests for 2, 5 and 10; numbers divisible by 2, 5 and 10; numbers divisible by 4; numbers divisible by 3 and 9; writing a natural number's prime factorisation; natural numbers; whole numbers; divisibility of natural numbers; greatest common divisor and least common multiple; prime and composite numbers; divisibility criteria.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 8 teacher-evidence items from 8 of the guides (Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 26, Book 29, Book 30; Grades 4, 5, 6).

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 7 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 8 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 6.

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

There is no teacher guide and no syllabus in the Moldova Maths set in hand, so every Moldova row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Moldova counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 394 teaching items. The expansion map's “12 student + 0 teacher” is correct: textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files reconcile exactly at 12 distinct works — Grades 3–12 (10 books) recorded in books.json, Grades 1–2 as git-blob files recorded in textbooks.tsv. The old closed-52 inventory's “Moldova link-only, 0 in hand” is refuted: all 12 books sit secured in the tensae-code/liqlearns-textbooks GitHub repo and were read from there for this expansion (Grades 3, 4 and 6–12 via the mid-size-books-8 release; Grades 1, 2 and 5 via the Contents API on their textbooks.tsv git-blob paths), byte-verified — MD5s match books.json exactly for the ten Grades 3–12 books. The Grades 10–12 books are dual-track (profil real / profil umanistic) throughout; each section is stocked once, as one Moldova row, not once per track. Book errors: none found that changes a placement; the Grade 9 file's PDF p. 178 carries a stray page from another book (a biology text page) bound after the back cover in the publisher's print file — a file artifact, not part of the mathematics manual, cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the “Cine seamănă culege” review blocks, the Recapitulare / sistematizare / evaluare sections, the “Să recapitulăm” summaries, the recap exercise sets and “Test sumativ” pages closing chapters, and the “Răspunsuri (și indicații)” answer keys — are stocked nowhere, except where a review section is a topic's only statement, which is then marked as such. ## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 9 requirement-evidence items from Book 2 and Book 4.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines; the held file is the July 2019 revision, footer “High School Mathematics Curriculum – The Bahamas, 2019”) — Bahamas, Department of Education; 3,766,723 bytes, 219 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 3 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 2 and Book 3.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard 1* (teacher guide, Grade 3) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 803,827 bytes, 62 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

**Book 3:** *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide, Standard IV* (teacher guide, Grade 6) — Belize, Ministry of Education, August 2012; 868,323 bytes, 51 PDF pages. Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–15; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 16–45. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series (M1.16 onwards). The detailed sections of this guide are markedly thinner than in Books 1–2 (several state “Specific Content: N/A”); placements rest on the outcomes as stated. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3.

**Book A1:** *Cambridge Mathematics 8 (CambridgeMaths GOLD)* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2012, ISBN 978-1-107-64383-3; 62,774,078 bytes, 554 PDF pp. The GOLD support edition of the Essential Mathematics Year 8 line, developed for students who require additional support in mathematics; a distinct work from Book A2 (different ISBN, extent and arrangement), not a duplicate file. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 14).

**Book A2:** *Essential Mathematics for the Australian Curriculum Year 8, Third Edition* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2019, ISBN 978-1-108-77281-5; 35,833,730 bytes, 846 PDF pp. The main Year 8 Australian Curriculum volume of the Essential Mathematics line (Book A1 is its Gold support sibling). Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18).

**Book A3:** *Essential Mathematics for the Victorian Curriculum Year 8, Third Edition* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2024, ISBN 978-1-009-48075-8; 24,871,876 bytes, 955 PDF pp. The Victorian Curriculum state edition of the Essential Mathematics Year 8 line — a distinct work from Book A2. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 20). Its contents lists Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the full chapter IS bound into this file, after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941), and is read and placed (flag F1).

## Costa Rica added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Costa Rica's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **25 books, all of them student books: the five CONED distance-secondary antologías *Antología de Matemáticas*, Niveles 7–11 (UNED/CONED — Nivel Sétimo 2017, Nivel Noveno 2018, Nivel 11 2019; Nivel Décimo by MSc. Jorge Díaz Porras) and the ITCR Proyecto EVEPRIM primary books for Grades 3 and 6 (10 textos + 10 folletos de prácticas across Relaciones y Álgebra, Números, Geometría, Medidas, Estadística and Probabilidad); 0 teacher guides for Costa Rica Maths exist in hand** — and placed 229 teaching items. **SET CORRECTION: the expansion map's count of 17 student is WRONG** — 17 is exactly the number of Costa Rica Mathematics rows in textbooks.tsv (10 EVEPRIM textos + only 2 of the 10 EVEPRIM folletos + 5 CONED antologías); the TSV was never updated for 8 folletos staged on 2026-10-25, and the staging books.json files and phase3-push-state.json agree on the true set of 25 (the folletos count student-side, per the map's own stated basis). All 25 were grabbed, verified (byte size + MD5 against books.json; the two Relaciones y Álgebra textos, which have no books.json entry, verified by size + title page as the main-repo files) and read in full — no partial excerpts, no duplicates (the SharePoint folder copies of the six texto books matched the staged sizes byte-for-byte, so they are the same files, cited once) and no unopenable files. The three Grade 3 textos were read in their November 2026 editions, the in-hand editions per books.json; the GitHub release mid-size-books-3 still holds the superseded October editions of those three textos (a record-level flag, reported, not fixed — same books, older exports; only the November editions are cited here). Two chapter promises in the CONED books are NOT teaching and place no item anywhere: CONED 7's Capítulo 4 is titled Estadística y Probabilidad but teaches statistics only (probability appears only in its introductory discussion questions, pp. 209–214), and CONED 10's Capítulo 1 objectives promise plane transformations that no Tema in the chapter teaches (only a recall of Grade 8 homothety). The placements' named exclusions — front matter, indices, bibliographies and answer keys (soluciones); the EVEPRIM answer-key chapters and the review chapters that re-teach already-placed sections; the CONED chapter motto/introduction pages and “desafío matemático” pages; and the videos and web interactives the EVEPRIM books link to — are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The 5 CONED book-error groups found in the read — CONED 7: the angle table (p. 106) labels a 360° angle “CONVEXO”, the inverse-proportionality example (pp. 201–203) states v·t = 247.5 for a product of 330 and “very close to 3 hours” at 78 km/h for ≈4.23 h with garbled fraction work, the triangle inequality (p. 125) omits “any two” sides, the median answer (p. 238) gives 68 for a median of 67, the worked powers page (p. 64) misaligns its results, and a tiling exercise (p. 35) reads “largo por largo”; CONED 8: the table of contents numbers the equations Tema “Tema 4” while the body heads it “Tema 5” (numbering slip only); CONED 9: the SI prefix table (p. 74) gives exa the symbol “R” (exa is E), the radian-to-degree rule (p. 117) is printed inverted/garbled though its worked examples are correct, and the contents duplicates “Función Cuadrática” for Capítulo 3 Temas 1 and 4 and mislabels Capítulo 4 as “Capítulo III”; CONED 10: the falling-body model (p. 373) prints “12” for ½ (the worked values use ½ correctly), the key (p. 42) duplicates “tangentes” where “secantes” is meant, p. 14 asks for the area of a “circunferencia” (the circle), and the pentagon solution (p. 86) drops its degree symbols; CONED 11: the logarithm table (p. 118) misaligns its examples (it prints log₅ 5 = 2, which belongs to log₅ 25), the wound-model solution (p. 172) shows an unsigned division display before the correct result, and “invectiva” (p. 121) is a typo for inyectiva — are carried corrected on the rows they touch. One further note is carried as the placements carry it: the Medidas G3 answer key's building-height estimate reads 45 dam in the October copy and “4 dam” in the November extraction — treated as a re-export/extraction artefact in the key, not teaching content, and cited nowhere. Costa Rica's books are pupil books, so Costa Rica is COUNTED: it joins this lesson's acceptance count wherever the placements stock an item. CONED printed page = PDF page throughout; EVEPRIM pages are cited by PDF page with the book's section numbers (two exceptions print folios: the EVEPRIM 6 Probabilidad texto's printed folio = PDF page − 6 and the EVEPRIM 3 Medidas texto's = PDF page − 7; citations give PDF pages). Into this lesson the placements stock 8 items (items 3, 4, 116, 139, 140, 150, 151 and 152).

**Book 2:** *Números, 6º — Folleto de prácticas* — Costa Rica, Escuela de Matemática, ITCR, Proyecto EVEPRIM 6; 192,462 bytes, 48 PDF pp.; a compilation of the practices of the Números texto (its own p. 2). Pages cited by PDF page. Pupil practice book.
